{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"06800674-bdb6-4fe1-bdcb-ad54e2d37b14","agentId":"aa0d91545d546b9dc","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Plex remote access is down because port 32400 is not forwarded on the AT&T BGW210 gateway (192.168.1.254). The BGW210 login requires a cookie dance + MD5(access_code+nonce)\n2. The access code is on the physical gateway label\n\nRead the credentials file for the BGW210 access code:\n/Volumes/synologyNFS/server-manager/secrets/credentials.md\n\nAlso read the network lessons for BGW210 details:\n/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager/memory/lessons/network.md\n\nThe BGW210 API approach:\n1. GET the login page to get the nonce/session cookie\n2. POST with MD5(access_code + nonce) to authenticate\n3. Use the firewall/NAT API to add the port forward rule\n\nIf the API approach doesn't work, try using curl to interact with the BGW210's REST-like endpoints. The gateway may have endpoints like:\n- /cgi-bin/firewall.ha\n- /cgi-bin/natgaming.ha  \n\nCheck what endpoints exist. Some BGW210 firmware versions expose a REST API at /xslt endpoints.\n\nIf all programmatic approaches fail, report exactly what we need the user to do manually (step by step for the BGW210 UI).\n\nAfter adding the port forward, verify by:\n1. Checking the BGW210 shows the rule\n2. Testing external reachability: curl to mardovplex.duckdns.org:32400 from outside (or use a web tool)\n3. {"parentUuid":"a47c2448-a280-4ac4-a724-6dd34d4bb219","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"06800674-bdb6-4fe1-bdcb-ad54e2d37b14","agentId":"aa0d91545d546b9dc","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UmrdZ3sDMLFMKQjqDzNfqB","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Lessons — Network & Routing\n     2→\n     3→### UniFi MCP Tool Loading Quirks (2026-02-21) {#unifi-mcp-tool-loading}\n     4→<!-- meta: domain=network,mcp | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-21 | keywords=UniFi,unifi_tool_index,unifi_execute,unifi_batch,unifi_load_tools,unifi_list_networks,unifi_list_clients,Unknown tool,write tools -->\n     5→- `unifi_tool_index` lists 86 tools but `unifi_execute` returns \"Unknown tool\" for many\n     6→- `unifi_load_tools` says it loaded them but `unifi_execute` and `unifi_batch` still can't find them\n     7→- `unifi_list_networks` and `unifi_list_clients` work via `unifi_execute` — basic read tools are fine\n     8→- Write tools like `unifi_set_client_ip_settings` and `unifi_update_network` do NOT work via execute or batch\n     9→- **Workaround**: For network config changes, use the UniFi web UI or direct REST API calls\n    10→- **Direct API method** (confirmed working 2026-03-13): Login via `POST /api/auth/login`, get CSRF token from JWT cookie, then use `GET/PUT /proxy/network/api/s/default/rest/networkconf/<id>` with full object. Must send complete object back (not partial update). CSRF header: `X-CSRF-Token: <token_from_jwt>`\n    11→- Successfully used this to enable mDNS + IGMP Snooping and create 3 firewall rules via `POST /proxy/network/api/s/default/rest/firewallrule`\n    12→- Firewall rule_index must be 20000+ (2000 gives \"FirewallRuleIndexOutOfRange\")\n    13→\n    14→### HA → Server Subnet Routing (2026-02-22) {#ha-server-subnet-routing}\n    15→<!-- meta: domain=network,home-assistant | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-22 | keywords=HAOS,server subnet,192.168.1.x,socat,TCP proxy,dual-homed,edna-core,192.168.0.210,GPU node -->\n    16→- HAOS cannot reach server subnet (192.168.1.x) — default route goes through IoT interface\n    17→- Affects: GPU node (192.168.1.67), and Ollama via server IP (192.168.1.201)\n    18→- **Workaround**: socat TCP proxy on dual-homed edna-core VM (has both subnet IPs)\n    19→- `socat TCP-LISTEN:<port>,fork,bind=0.0.0.0,reuseaddr TCP:<target_host>:<target_port>`\n    20→- HA then connects via edna-core's IoT IP (192.168.0.210)\n    21→\n    22→### UniFi mDNS Relay for Sonos Cross-VLAN Discovery (2026-03-13) {#unifi-mdns-sonos-vlan}\n    23→<!-- meta: domain=network,sonos | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-13 | keywords=mDNS,Multicast DNS,IoT Auto-Discovery,Sonos,VLAN,cross-subnet,IGMP,UniFi,UDM Pro,reflector,multicast-relay -->\n    24→- **UI path (UniFi Network 8.x)**: Settings → Networks → edit each network → enable \"Multicast DNS\" (also called \"IoT Auto-Discovery\") toggle\n    25→- Must enable on BOTH the source network (192.168.1.x / Main) AND the destination network (192.168.0.x / IoT) — one-sided does not work\n    26→- Also enable \"IGMP Snooping\" on both networks to reduce multicast flooding from Sonos\n    27→- **mDNS Proxy mode** (newer firmware): Settings → Networks → mDNS Proxy → set to \"All\" or \"Custom\" (Custom lets you pick specific service types like AirPlay)\n    28→- **Critical gotcha**: Built-in UniFi mDNS reflector is \"all or nothing\" — reflects between ALL enabled networks, including WAN. Cannot be scoped to just two VLANs.\n    29→- **Sonos-specific gotcha**: Sonos also uses SSDP (UDP 1900) for speaker-to-speaker discovery. mDNS relay alone is not sufficient for full Sonos functionality — SSDP must also pass between VLANs.\n    30→- **Firewall rules required** if IoT VLAN is isolated: allow Sonos IP range → Main network on TCP 3400/3401/3500 and UDP 319/1900/1901/1902/6969/32768-65535 (LAN In, before predefined rules). Also allow established/related sessions.\n    31→- **Multicast to Unicast pitfall**: if enabled on the IoT WiFi network (Settings → WiFi → Advanced → Hi-Capacity Tuning), it breaks mDNS. Disable it.\n    32→- **STP pitfall**: Sonos uses pre-standard STP path costs, incompatible with UniFi RSTP default. Disable STP on switch ports connected to wired Sonos speakers (Settings → Devices → Port Manager → port → Advanced → disable STP).\n    33→- **Fallback if built-in reflector fails**: run `multicast-relay` Python script or Docker container on UDM Pro to relay between specific bridge interfaces (e.g., `br0` and `br0.vlan_id`). Survives firmware updates only if persisted via on-boot script.\n    34→- **CLI/API**: No documented CLI flag for mDNS reflector on UDM Pro. Enable via web UI or UniFi Network REST API (`PUT /api/s/default/rest/networkconf/<network_id>` with `mdns_enabled: true`).\n    35→\n    36→### Inter-Subnet Routing: 1.x Cannot Reach 0.x (2026-03-13) {#inter-vlan-tcp-blocked}\n    37→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=3 | last_triggered=2026-03-13 | keywords=inter-VLAN,TCP,ICMP,ping,SSH,timeout,UniFi,BGW320,AT&T,vmbr0,vmbr1,static route,192.168.1.254,192.168.1.91 -->\n    38→- **Root cause**: AT&T BGW320 gateway (192.168.1.254) has no route to 192.168.0.0/24 — drops ALL traffic (ICMP and TCP), not just TCP\n    39→- Previous diagnosis blaming SynologyRouter was wrong — BGW320 is the actual blocker\n    40→- UniFi has ZERO firewall rules — completely clean, not involved\n    41→- The 0.x subnet lives behind the UDM Pro (192.168.1.91), but the BGW320 doesn't know that\n    42→- Earlier belief that \"ICMP works but TCP doesn't\" was false — testing was done from the Mac (which is itself on 0.x)\n    43→- **Fix (recommended)**: Static route on each 1.x VM: `ip route add 192.168.0.0/24 via 192.168.1.91`\n    44→- Make permanent via netplan: `routes: [{to: 192.168.0.0/24, via: 192.168.1.91}]`\n    45→- **Alternative**: Continue dual-homing with vmbr1 NIC (current workaround for HA VM 100, edna-core VM 150)\n    46→- **Alternative**: Static route on SynologyRouter (192.168.1.64) to cover all hosts routing through it\n    47→\n    48→### AT&T BGW210 Network Topology & Port Forwarding (2026-03-18) {#att-bgw210-topology}\n    49→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=2 | last_triggered=2026-03-18 | keywords=AT&T,BGW210,topology,Synology Router,transparent bridge,NAS,Proxmox,UniFi,port forward,NAT,double-NAT,plex -->\n    50→- **Model**: BGW210-700 (not BGW320 as previously noted)\n    51→- NAS (Synology) and Proxmox on 192.168.1.x connect directly to BGW210 — they bypass UniFi\n    52→- Synology Router at .64 is in **bridge mode** (confirmed via SRM API: `net_topology: \"bridge\"`) — does NOT do NAT or port forwarding\n    53→- UniFi UDM Pro (192.168.1.91) WAN IP is 192.168.1.91 (private!) — **double-NAT**\n    54→- **Default Server (DMZ) configured on BGW210** → UDM Pro (192.168.1.91). All unsolicited inbound goes to UniFi.\n    55→- Port forwards can go on UniFi OR BGW210. Belt-and-suspenders: add to BOTH for critical services.\n    56→- IP Passthrough does NOT persist on this firmware — use Default Server mode instead (see lesson #bgw210-ip-passthrough-resets)\n    57→- **BGW210 login flow** (JS-rendered, requires cookie dance):\n    58→  1. `curl -c cookies http://192.168.1.254/` (get session cookie)\n    59→  2. `curl -b cookies http://192.168.1.254/cgi-bin/apphosting.ha` (extract nonce from hidden field)\n    60→  3. Compute `MD5(access_code + nonce)` and POST to `/cgi-bin/login.ha`\n    61→  4. NAT/Gaming page: `/cgi-bin/apphosting.ha` — select service + device MAC, submit Add\n    62→- **Custom services**: `/cgi-bin/services.ha` — define port ranges before adding NAT rules\n    63→- Access code stored in credentials.md (not AT&T account password — separate device access code)\n    64→- PlexServer service (TCP/UDP 32400) already existed; rule was pointing to wrong device (\"ubuntu-server\"). Fixed to point to 192.168.1.200 MAC `82:ef:62:0e:2b:f4`\n    65→\n    66→### Tailscale IP for Both Local and Remote Access (2026-03-17) {#tailscale-ip-unified}\n    67→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-17 | keywords=Tailscale,local,remote,LAN,tunnel,unified address,service config -->\n    68→- Tailscale IPs route locally on LAN (direct connection) and tunnel when remote (via relay/DERP)\n    69→- Use the Tailscale IP as a single address in service configs — works from both local and remote locations\n    70→- Eliminates need for separate local/remote URLs or VPN configurations\n    71→\n    72→### UniFi API Cannot Modify Network Config (2026-03-17) {#unifi-api-network-config-403}\n    73→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-17 | keywords=UniFi,API,403,401,network config,Internet 2,WAN2,disable,web UI,networkevie,admin -->\n    74→- UniFi API with `networkevie` credentials: authenticates and reads fine, but returns 403 on network config modifications (disable WAN2, etc.)\n    75→- Admin credentials don't authenticate via API at all (401)\n    76→- **Root cause**: UDM Pro may require \"Super Admin\" or \"Network Admin\" role for WAN/Internet config changes\n    77→- **Workaround**: Must use UniFi web UI (192.168.1.91) for network configuration changes like disabling Internet 2\n    78→- This is a UDM Pro restriction, not a general UniFi API limitation — firewall rules CAN be created via API\n    79→\n    80→### WAN2 Flapping Breaks WebSocket Apps (2026-03-17) {#wan2-flapping-websocket}\n    81→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-17 | keywords=WAN2,Internet 2,flapping,Venmo,WebSocket,source IP,failover,UDM Pro,Jan 30 -->\n    82→- UDM Pro default config auto-detects WAN2 port and enables Internet 2 failover\n    83→- If WAN2 port has signal noise (even without a real ISP connection), it flaps between WAN1/WAN2\n    84→- WAN flapping changes source IP mid-session, killing WebSocket-dependent apps (Venmo, banking apps)\n    85→- Started Jan 30 2026 — no config change found in logs; likely physical signal noise on WAN2 port\n    86→- **Fix**: Disable Internet 2 in UniFi UI → Settings → Internet → Internet 2 → Disable\n    87→- Works fine on cellular because phone uses a single consistent IP\n    88→\n    89→### Cross-Subnet File Transfer Routing (2026-03-17) {#cross-subnet-file-transfer}\n    90→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-17 | keywords=cross-subnet,SCP,file transfer,relay,intermediate node,192.168.0.x,192.168.1.x -->\n    91→- Mac on IoT subnet (192.168.0.x) cannot SCP directly to devices on 192.168.1.x (no route through BGW320)\n    92→- **Fix**: Use an intermediate dual-homed node (e.g. edna-core, which has IPs on both subnets) as a relay point\n    93→- Alternative: Copy to NFS share from one subnet, read from the other\n    94→\n    95→### World Anvil Cloudflare Bypass (2026-03-17) {#worldanvil-cloudflare-bypass}\n    96→<!-- meta: domain=network,scraping | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-17 | keywords=World Anvil,Cloudflare,cf_clearance,Playwright,cloudscraper,fetch MCP,scraping -->\n    97→- Playwright (headless or headed) always hits Cloudflare \"Just a moment...\" challenge on worldanvil.com\n    98→- cf_clearance cookie is fingerprint-locked to the browser that solved the challenge — copying it to Playwright/curl doesn't work\n    99→- **Fix**: Use `cloudscraper` Python library (handles JS challenges automatically) OR the MCP fetch tool (bypasses Cloudflare)\n   100→- World Anvil has internal AJAX APIs: `/w/{slug}/load/categories` returns JSON with article links, `/load/category/{uuid}` for sub-categories\n   101→- World Anvil search API: `/api/worlds/{world-uuid}/articles?term=X&type=advanced`\n   102→- Built `tools/worldanvil-fetch-scrape.py` using cloudscraper + BeautifulSoup as the working solution\n   103→\n   104→### BGW210 IP Passthrough Does NOT Persist (2026-03-18) {#bgw210-ip-passthrough-resets}\n   105→<!-- meta: domain=network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-18 | keywords=AT&T,BGW210,IP Passthrough,DHCPS-fixed,Default Server,DMZ,firmware,persist,broadband restart -->\n   106→- BGW210-700 IP Passthrough (Firewall → IP Passthrough → allocmode=passthrough) **reverts to Off** on every broadband restart\n   107→- Confirmed: saved correctly, verified, but after broadband restart the setting resets silently\n   108→- This is a known firmware limitation on some BGW210 builds\n   109→- **Workaround**: Use \"Default Server\" mode instead (allocmode=defaultserver, defsrvint=192.168.1.91)\n   110→- Default Server forwards ALL unsolicited inbound traffic to the UDM Pro — port forwards then live on UniFi\n   111→- Belt-and-suspenders: ALSO add explicit NAT/Gaming rules on the BGW210 for critical ports (32400 for Plex)\n   112→- IP Passthrough page: `/cgi-bin/ippass.ha`; Default Server just needs an IP, not a MAC\n   113→\n   114→### UDM Pro API Reboot with CSRF (2026-03-18) {#udm-pro-api-reboot-csrf}\n   115→<!-- meta: domain=network,mcp | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-18 | keywords=UniFi,UDM Pro,reboot,restart,API,CSRF,JWT,devmgr,unifi_reboot_device -->\n   116→- `unifi_reboot_device` via MCP execute returns \"Unknown tool\" — not loaded or broken\n   117→- **Direct API method** (confirmed working):\n   118→  1. Login: `POST https://192.168.1.91/api/auth/login` with JSON credentials\n   119→  2. Extract CSRF from JWT: decode TOKEN cookie payload, read `csrfToken` field\n   120→  3. Restart: `POST /proxy/network/api/s/default/cmd/devmgr` with `{\"cmd\":\"restart\",\"mac\":\"<device_mac>\"}` and `X-CSRF-Token` header\n   121→- UDM Pro management MAC: `74:ac:b9:de:1e:82`; WAN port MAC: `74:ac:b9:de:1e:8a` (different!)\n   122→- JWT base64 decode needs padding fix: add `=` chars to make length divisible by 4\n   123→\n   124→<!-- lesson: 182 | trigger_count: 0 | last: 2026-03-18 -->\n   125→## 182. World Anvil scrape JSON uses content_html not content\n   126→The worldanvil-scrape.py output uses keys: name, url, scraped_at, title, content_html, content_text, sidebar, images. The content field is NOT present — use content_text first, then strip HTML from content_html as fallback.\n   127→\n   128→"}]},"uuid":"e72638fb-a3a3-4892-960d-8a429293a093","timestamp":"2026-03-18T21:04:48.840Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a47c2448-a280-4ac4-a724-6dd34d4bb219","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"9a0336f9-f979-4e7a-9708-e45db33ce573","version":"2.1.78","gitBranch":"main","slug":"elegant-launching-giraffe"}

{"parentUuid":"6883d04e-56be-4f96-a613-54d5aee0ff84","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aa0d91545d546b9dc","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_017gQ84EsDvQbYrBoRHwRFL8","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_016rK2yoAdTWqf581Po5egnZ","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"# Extract CSRF token from JWT cookie, then check port forwarding rules\npython3 << 'PYEOF'\nimport subprocess, json, base64, re\n\n# Get CSRF from JWT\nwith open('/tmp/unifi_c.txt') as f:\n    cookies = f.read()\n\n# Find TOKEN cookie\ntoken_m = re.search(r'TOKEN\\s+(\\S+)', cookies)\nif not token_m:\n    print(\"No TOKEN cookie found\")\n    print(cookies)\n    exit(1)\n\ntoken = token_m.group(1)\n# Decode JWT payload\nparts = token.split('.')\npayload = parts[1]\npadding = 4 - len(payload) % 4\nif padding != 4:\n    payload += '=' * padding\ndecoded = json.loads(base64.b64decode(payload))\ncsrf = decoded.get('csrfToken', '')\nprint(f\"CSRF: {csrf}\")\n\n# Get port forwarding rules\nr = subprocess.run(\n    ['curl', '-s', '-k', '-b', '/tmp/unifi_c.txt',\n     '-H', f'X-CSRF-Token: {csrf}',\n     'https://192.168.1.91/proxy/network/api/s/default/rest/portforward',\n     '-o', '/tmp/unifi_portfwd.json'],\n    capture_output=True, text=True\n)\nwith open('/tmp/unifi_portfwd.json') as f:\n    data = json.load(f)\nprint(f\"Port forward rules: {data.get('meta', {})}\")\nrules = data.get('data', [])\nprint(f\"Count: {len(rules)}\")\nfor rule in rules:\n    print(f\"  - {rule.get('name','?')}: {rule.get('proto','?')} {rule.get('dst_port','?')} -> {rule.get('fwd','?')}:{rule.get('fwd_port','?')} enabled={rule.get('enabled','?')}\")\nPYEOF","description":"Get UniFi port forwarding rules via API"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3772,"cache_read_input_tokens":53383,"output_tokens":570,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3772},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[],"speed":"standard"}},"requestId":"req_011CZBERs7Xtqv9EHbDc7nva","type":"assistant","uuid":"720e0b9a-fca6-4bae-a1e4-3366ecfbef69","timestamp":"2026-03-18T21:10:50.943Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"9a0336f9-f979-4e7a-9708-e45db33ce573","version":"2.1.78","gitBranch":"main","slug":"elegant-launching-giraffe"}

{"parentUuid":"c19a8c57-dc1b-4868-81c0-35621f8f5bba","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aa0d91545d546b9dc","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_01Laf9CVPWTod6vyuLcZTUKm","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I can see the PlexServer rule in the BGW210 is correctly pointing to 192.168.1.200. The reply from 192.168.1.200 would need to go back through the BGW210. So traffic for port 32400 should go directly to 192.168.1.200, not to the UDM Pro. Check the BGW210 firewall/packet filter page to see if there are any block rules\npython3 << 'PYEOF'\nimport subprocess, re, hashlib, urllib.parse\n\naccess_code = '3/#?3*/*@4'\n\n# Login\nsubprocess.run(['curl', '-s', '-c', '/tmp/bgw_fw.txt', 'http://192.168.1.254/', '-o', '/dev/null', '-L'], capture_output=True)\nsubprocess.run(['curl', '-s', '-b', '/tmp/bgw_fw.txt', '-c', re.search(r'name=[\"\\']?nonce[\"\\']?\\s+value=[\"\\']?([a-f0-9]+)[\"\\']?', c).group(1)\nmd5 = hashlib.md5((access_code + nonce).encode()).hexdigest()\npost = urllib.parse.urlencode({'nonce': nonce, {"parentUuid":"d186011f-5ce1-45ad-b82d-4f5584c5a571","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aa0d91545d546b9dc","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_01WTexTRKJZYPDQdvUPHYsgD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Here's the full diagnosis and outcome:\n\n---\n\n**Plex remote access is working.** Port 32400 is open and reachable externally.\n\n## What I found\n\n**BGW210 state (already correct):**\n- NAT/Gaming rule: `PlexServer TCP/UDP 32400 → 192.168.1.200` — present and correctly configured\n- Default Server mode: `→ 192.168.1.91 (UDM Pro)` — active, but specific NAT rules take precedence\n- IP Passthrough: Off\n\n**UniFi state (already correct):**\n- Port forward: `Plex Media Server TCP/UDP 32400 → 192.168.1.200:32400 enabled=True`\n\n**External verification:**\n- `portchecker.io` confirmed port 32400 is open: `\"status\":true`\n- `curl http://mardovplex.duckdns.org:32400/identity` returns `HTTP 200 OK`\n- `plex.tv` cloud API shows `myPlexMappingState: mapped`, `publicAddress: 99.112.162.67`, `presence: 1`\n\n**The `publicAddress: None` from the local Plex API** was a red herring — the local endpoint returns an empty string for that field, but plex.tv knows the correct public IP. Remote access was already functioning.\n\n## One thing I did fix (auth method)\n\nThe existing lesson had the wrong field name for the BGW210 login. The form uses `hashpassword` (not `password`) for the MD5 hash. Using `password` redirects back to login; using `hashpassword` succeeds. I've updated the network lesson with the corrected flow so future automation works first-try."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":844,"cache_read_input_tokens":78446,"output_tokens":418,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":844},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[],"speed":"standard"}},"requestId":"req_011CZBEmxLcx8v1NESuH38x8","type":"assistant","uuid":"ea200728-237a-4d66-8be9-507a33bea028","timestamp":"2026-03-18T21:15:24.414Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"9a0336f9-f979-4e7a-9708-e45db33ce573","version":"2.1.78","gitBranch":"main","slug":"elegant-launching-giraffe"}
